# Chinese 07 — Advanced lexical, rhetorical, and source precision

This is the 1,800-card part A assigned by [execution #39](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/issues/39), within [Chinese plan #3](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/issues/3) and [roadmap #1](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/issues/1). The source is Simplified Chinese, zh-Hans-CN, with standard spoken Chinese (Putonghua) production. Chinese 00–06 contributes 5,200 unchanged cards/maps; Chinese 00–07 totals 7,000. This does not complete the ten-stage, 10,000-card language.

The pinned [guide §6.7](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/blob/e1d8239b7560f26b092cc7bb97c005738b34361f/languages/zh.md#67-proficient-integration), together with its shared systems and pronunciation/lexical support sections, supplies the curricular categories. The following ranges identify actual hidden learning, rather than incidental visible terminology.

| Assigned part-A coverage | Hidden IDs | Relation to accepted learning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Advanced general, social, institutional, narrative/nonfiction, cultural and intellectual lexical senses and collocations | zh-07-0001–0380, block A (380) | Builds on accepted 05–06 lexical breadth through new senses, independent lexical forms, first focused retrieval and honestly identified counterparts. |
| Specialist/professional explanations across research/work, services, education and public/community responsibilities | zh-07-0381–0650, block B (270) | Actual specialist circumstances distinguish ordinary and technical frames; no professional noun is counted as a newly taught familiar grammar construction. |
| Multiple-source relations, synthesis, attribution, competing explanations, argument and analytical evidence | zh-07-0651–0900, block C (250) | Extends accepted 04/06 source qualification with complete short sources, stated populations/units and explicit limits; relay is distinguished from independent corroboration and inference from observation. |
| Argument structure, rhetorical selection, limitations, reformulation and calibrated stance | zh-07-0901–1140, block D (240) | Premises, inference, force, qualification and deliberate changes are visible; lexical counterparts for known reasoning relations are identified as such. |
| Formal lexical/constructional frames, writing precision and audience fit | zh-07-1141–1370, block E (230) | Builds on accepted 06 written/spoken distinctions with actual actors, audiences, time relations and commitments. |
| Advanced whole idioms, four-character expressions and collocations | zh-07-1371–1590, block F (220) | Whole selected readings, contemporary meaning, literal association, syntax/collocation, register and unsuitable contexts have expression-specific support. Four characters alone do not establish a chengyu; unnecessary origin narratives are omitted. |
| Whole-word pronunciation/lexical choices, interaction, source-preserving paraphrase and precision editing | zh-07-1591–1800, block G (210) | 1591–1640 focuses whole lexical readings and selected senses, including neutral/polyphonic contrasts; 1641–1800 supplies actual group exchanges, attention/entry, repair/topic/stance and complete originals/results for precise text operations. Known pronunciation principles and earlier interaction concepts are not renamed as new principles. |

G’s hidden components are located precisely: zh-07-1591–1640 covers whole lexical readings and selected senses; zh-07-1669–1677 covers pronunciation, delivery and style terminology; zh-07-1678–1680 covers interrupted continuity, resumption and scene movement. Actual turn components include zh-07-1661 (attentive listening), 1663 (question entry and floor return), 1665 (interruption and repair), 1667–1668 (topic uptake and waiting for an utterance to end), 1681 (topic change), and 1713–1714 (resumed discussion and deliberation). Separate attributed reporting appears at 1709–1710; calculation checking at 1715; extraction, faithful revision and annotation at 1718–1745; problem, meaning and argument checks at 1746–1748; backward/source checking and group review at 1773–1780 and 1800. The remaining G rows add lexical uses in reflection, decisions, participation and responses to style. These focused components do not claim whole-text mastery or completion of stage 08.

Each row gives actual strictly earlier Chinese dependencies or a self-contained introduction. Material comparisons use the actual earlier sense/frame and source/map, not merely a shared topic or stage heading. A new lexical form may retrieve a familiar concept; first focused retrieval after visible support is distinguished from a genuinely new sense. Spanish's accepted 7,600 cards remain an unchanged project-order dependency, not assumed Chinese learner knowledge.

Actual roles: **P 1800; R 0**. Recognition IDs: none. There is no numeric recognition quota.

Contemporary standard formal language can remain P. Only F permits genuinely marked R, with actual attributed marked wording and audience on the front, substantive Source/register attribution, and a compatible natural Simplified hidden interpretation. Accepted Chinese 00–05 remains P-only; accepted 06's eight F-only R cards are unchanged. This stage introduces neither Traditional nor regional production.

Stage 08 retains its entire unchanged 1,800-card §6.7 part B: mediation/translation and cross-viewpoint synthesis; literary interpretation, layered stance, diction and viewpoint; verified historical/cultural/literary references; literary/social idioms and internet/media language; regional/dialect-influenced recognition and sensitivities; passive Traditional-character recognition with Simplified responses; and further integrated discourse/style work. Part-A lexical prerequisites, short original figurative examples and focused group components do not complete those later allocations. The final shared 07/08 reconciliation remains with 08; whole-language reconciliation remains after 09.

The advanced working landmarks remain at least 3,000 recognized characters, at least 11,000 words/phrases and approximately 1,200 handwritten characters. Separately, the guide cites advanced anchors of 3,000 characters, 11,092 words/phrases, 572 grammar points and 1,200 handwritten characters. These are retained guide references, not newly verified official inventories or evidence that 7,000 cards meet the landmarks.

Exact source-string measures on the final editorial text:

| Measure | 07 | 00–07 |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Cards | 1,800 | 7,000 |
| Mappings | 1,800 | 7,000 |
| Distinct NFC exact clozes | 1,799 | 6,679 |
| Distinct NFC exact lemmas | 1,799 | 6,673 |
| Exact authored lemma/meaning pairs | 1,800 | 6,999 |
| Han D: hidden clozes | 1,420 | 2,235 |
| Han S: whole sentences | 2,252 | 2,642 |
| Han V: own cloze removed | 1,862 | 2,068 |
| Han I: S minus D | 832 | 407 |
| Han V intersect D | 1,030 | 1,661 |

Han uses U+3400–U+4DBF and U+4E00–U+9FFF. D unions hidden-cloze Han; S unions whole-sentence Han; V removes each sentence’s own single cloze before union; I=S−D. V may overlap D. English, Pinyin and back-side support are excluded. Attributed marked input contributes to S/V and only to D if hidden elsewhere. Exact authored pairs distinguish strings, not independently verified semantic concepts. These source-position measures do not perform word segmentation or establish pedagogically targeted visible recognition, vocabulary knowledge, recognized-character attainment, handwriting, examination performance or proficiency.

The table below uses the ordinary ten-column contract. Editorial accepted/none marks completed editorial resolution; it does not assert ORCHESTRATOR acceptance.
